Q1. A contract payment of ₹9000 is shared among A, B and C in the ratio 30:30:12. What amount goes to C?
A. ₹1500
B. ₹1502
C. ₹1875
D. ₹1495
Answer: ₹1500
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_wage_with_helper
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use contribution ratio for the share.
\[
Share=\frac{12}{72}\times 9000
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=1500
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹1500

---

Q2. 16 workers working 6 hours per day finish the job in 12 days. If the daily working time is 8 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 18 days?
A. 8 workers
B. 11 workers
C. 16 workers
D. 24 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
16\times 12\times 6=M_2\times 18\times 8
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{16\times 12\times 6}{18\times 8}=8
\]
Answer: 8 workers

---

Q3. A job can be finished in 24 days. If work continues for 2 days, what fraction is still incomplete?
A. 11/1
B. 12/11
C. 1/12
D. 11/12
Answer: 11/12
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
R=1-\frac{2}{24}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
R=\frac{22}{24}=11/12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{24-2}{24}=11/12
\]
Answer: 11/12

---

Q4. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. After 2 hours, a leak begins which empties a full tank in 30 hours. Find the total time to fill the tank?
A. 23 2/3 hours
B. 18 2/3 hours
C. 15 2/3 hours
D. 14 hours
Answer: 18 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_drain_after_partial_fill
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 2/3 hours

---

Q5. A, B and C together take 8 days. A and B together take 12 days. How long will C take alone?
A. 22 days
B. 19 1/5 days
C. 28 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_pairwise_total_abc
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_X=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q6. An examiner checks 24 answer sheets in 6 days. How many answer sheets are checked per day?
A. 6 answer sheets
B. 4 answer sheets
C. 4.8 answer sheets
D. 0.5 answer sheets
Answer: 4 answer sheets
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_reverse_contribution_deduction
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r=\frac{W}{t}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
r=\frac{24}{6}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
r=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
r=W/t
\]
Answer: 4 answer sheets

Q7. A and B work together at first. A's time is 20 days and B's time is 12 days. If A leaves after 2 days, when will the job be finished from the start?
A. 10 4/5 days
B. 9 4/5 days
C. 8.64 days
D. 16 4/5 days
Answer: 10 4/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(9+15)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{132}{15}=10.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 10 4/5 days

---

Q8. Workers can complete a job in 15 days if there is no break. Work is paused for 3 days. What is the total elapsed time?
A. 19 days
B. 23 days
C. 18 days
D. 21 3/5 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{405}{18+9}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 18 days

---

Q9. A pipe supplies 10 litres of water per minute for 5 minutes. How many litres of water are supplied?
A. 50 litres
B. 40 litres
C. 54 litres
D. 51 litres
Answer: 50 litres
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_overflow_waste_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
V_w=r\times t
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
V_w=10\times 5
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
V_w=50
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
V_w=r\times t_e
\]
Answer: 50 litres

Q10. Three workers take turns in the order A, B, C. They finish 8, 5 and 5 files on their turns. How many days are needed for 60 files?
A. 13 3/4 days
B. 14.63 days
C. 9 3/4 days
D. 11 3/4 days
Answer: 9 3/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+5+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=9.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 9 3/4 days

---

Q11. The working capacities of A and B are in the ratio 24:10. What is the ratio of the times taken by A and B for the same work?
A. 34:10
B. 10:34
C. 5:12
D. 24:10
Answer: 5:12
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T_A:T_B=10:24
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_A:T_B=5:12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5:12
\]
Answer: 5:12

Q12. A team repairs 12 metres daily, but another effect damages 3 metres daily. How many days are needed to complete 120 metres?
A. 15 1/3 days
B. 18 1/3 days
C. 13 1/3 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{120}{12-3}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q13. Food is sufficient for 20 people for 24 days. For how many days will the same food last for 40 people?
A. 13 days
B. 18 days
C. 12 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 24=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 24}{40}
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q17. A work order has 120 forms. The first team completes 17 forms per day for 3 days, then the changed team completes 20 per day. What is the completion time?
A. 12.45 days
B. 8.06 days
C. 9.45 days
D. 6.45 days
Answer: 6.45 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_replacement_worker
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
r_1t_1+r_2t_2=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=17\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{69}{20}=6.45
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 6.45 days

Q18. Food for 30 people lasts 24 days. If after 5 days the number of people changes to 35, how many more days will it last?

D. 13.29 days
Answer: 16.29 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:

\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720-30\times 5}{35}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=16.29
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 16.29 days

Q20. If \(\frac{3}{8}\) of a piece of work takes 9 days, how long will the entire work take?
A. 3.38 days
B. 17 days
C. 24 days
D. 72 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_fraction_days
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=9\times \frac{8}{3}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=9\div \frac{3}{8}=24
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q24. Three machines work in parallel and make 12, 15 and 18 parts per hour. For 6 hours, what is the total number of parts?
A. 270 items
B. 324 items
C. 275 items
D. 268 items
Answer: 270 items
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_parallel_machine_batches
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 6+15\times 6+18\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=270
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 270 items

Q25. A team of 12 workers has completed 96 items from a 240-item order. With 8 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 6 workers
B. 4.8 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 4 workers
Answer: 6 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{144}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 6 workers

Q27. A pipe fills a tank in 12 hours. If the tank is already \(\frac{1}{4}\) full, how many hours are needed to fill it completely?

D. 11 1/4 hours
Answer: 9 hours

\[

\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

Q30. Two filling pipes A and B take 12 and 18 hours respectively. If pipe B is opened 2 hours later, what is the time in hours to fill the tank?
A. 8 hours
B. 14 hours
C. 6 2/5 hours
D. 5 hours
Answer: 8 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_multiple_pipes_timing
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{30}{3+2}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Answer: 8 hours

Q36. A and B can separately complete a work in 15 and 30 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 9 1/4 days
B. 10 1/3 days
C. 7 1/3 days
D. 12 1/3 days
Answer: 12 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
12(T-2)+6(T-3)=180
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=12\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 12 1/3 days

---

Q38. A page job is shared by three typists. Their hourly page counts are 12, 15 and 18. What is the total number of pages in 5 hours?

B. 229 pages

D. 180 pages
Answer: 225 pages

Family: tw_typist_pages_per_hour
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 5+15\times 5+18\times 5
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=225
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 225 pages

Q39. Pipe A can fill a tank in 12 hours and pipe B can fill it in 18 hours. Pipe C can empty the tank in 36 hours. If all three pipes are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be filled?

\]

Q41. A works first and B works next, then the order repeats. A completes 10 forms and B completes 6 forms on their turns. How many hours are needed for 60 forms?

\[

\]
Answer: 7 1/3 hours

Q42. 8 workers have done 48 files out of 360. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?

\[

\]

Q43. A tank is filled by a pipe in 15 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 21 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?

D. 52 1/2 hours

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 52 1/2 hours

Q44. 15 workers finish a job in 16 days, working 6 hours daily. How many workers should work 8 hours daily to finish it in 12 days?
A. 20 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 15 workers
D. 11 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q49. If A can complete a work in 18 days and B can complete it in 6 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?

B. 12 days
C. 4 1/2 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 4 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[

\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=18/18=1,\quad B=18/6=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{18}{1+3}=4.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{18\times 6}{18+6}=4.5
\]
Answer: 4 1/2 days

Share=3600
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: ₹3600

Q80. A takes 10 days to finish a work and B takes 30 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 40 days
B. 7 1/2 days
C. 20 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

Put the given values into that relation.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_X=17.14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 17.14 days

W_1=6\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{150}{16}=14.38
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 14.38 days

Q189. A takes 15 days to finish a work and B takes 20 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?

B. 35 days
C. 17 1/2 days
D. 8.57 days
Answer: 8.57 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(15,20)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/15=4,\quad B=60/20=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{4+3}=8.57
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 20}{15+20}=8.57
\]
Answer: 8.57 days

Q190. A work requires 12 workers for 10 days with 8 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 16 days with 6 hours per day?

\[

\]

\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]

\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

D=T_{actual}-T_{planned}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
R=300-12\times 8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
D=\frac{204}{10}-\frac{204}{12}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 3 2/5 days

Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/15=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+2}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 15}{10+15}=6
\]
Answer: 6 days

---

Q262. 18 workers complete the same job in 8 days at 5 hours a day. What number of workers will complete it in 12 days at 6 hours a day?
A. 13 workers
B. 27 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q263. A filling pipe fills a tank in 12 hours and a leak empties it in 36 hours. How long will it take to fill the tank when both work together?
A. 10.57 hours
B. 6.86 hours
C. 14.57 hours
D. 8.57 hours
Answer: 8.57 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=8.57
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 8.57 hours

\[

\]

\[

Q299. A road crew lays 15 metres of road per day, while rain damages 3 metres per day. If 150 metres must be completed, in how many days will the road be finished?
A. 10 1/2 days
B. 12 1/2 days
C. 15 days
D. 17 1/2 days
Answer: 12 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{150}{15-3}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=12.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

Q300. A food stock lasts 30 days for 20 people. How many days will it last for 25 people?

\]

Q316. Without leakage, a tank fills in 15 hours. With leakage, it fills in 20 hours. Find the leak's emptying time in hours?
A. 60 hours
B. 57 hours
C. 48 hours
D. 66 hours
Answer: 60 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 60 hours

Q319. A and B can separately complete a job in 6 days and 20 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 6 days
B. 13 days
C. 26 days
D. 4.62 days
Answer: 4.62 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,20)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/6=10,\quad B=60/20=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{10+3}=4.62
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 20}{6+20}=4.62
\]
Answer: 4.62 days

Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=17
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 17 workers

\]

\]
Answer: 36 hours

Q346. A takes 8 days to finish a work and B takes 12 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?

Explanation:

\[

\]

\]

\]

\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{250}{15+10}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+1=11
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 11 days

---

Q393. A tank is filled by a pipe in 10 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 16 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?
A. 26 2/3 hours
B. 31 2/3 hours
C. 20 hours
D. 25 2/3 hours
Answer: 26 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]

\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{16}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/3 hours

Q396. A alone finishes a job in 30 days, while B alone finishes it in 18 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 48 days
B. 11 1/4 days
C. 18 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 11 1/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(30,18)=90
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=90/30=3,\quad B=90/18=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{90}{3+5}=11.25
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 18}{30+18}=11.25
\]
Answer: 11 1/4 days

\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{6}:\frac{1}{20}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
W_A:W_B=20:6
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
W_A:W_B=10:3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=10:3
\]
Answer: 10:3

Q410. A team processes 12 files per day for the first 5 days. After that, more workers join and the team processes 37 files per day. If 180 files must be completed, how many days are needed in all?

\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q411. A alone finishes a job in 6 days, while B alone finishes it in 10 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 6 days
B. 8 days
C. 16 days
D. 3 3/4 days
Answer: 3 3/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,10)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/10=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+3}=3.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 10}{6+10}=3.75
\]
Answer: 3 3/4 days

Q412. A tank is filled by a pipe taking 12 hours and emptied by another pipe taking 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be filled if both are opened?
A. 32 hours
B. 30 hours
C. 25 hours
D. 37 1/2 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

20\times 36=30\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 36}{30}
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q460. If A can complete a work in 6 days and B can complete it in 12 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 9 days
B. 6 days
C. 4 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,12)=12
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=12/6=2,\quad B=12/12=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{12}{2+1}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 12}{6+12}=4
\]
Answer: 4 days

Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+0
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q484. A team can complete a job in 10 days. If it works for 3 days, what fraction of the job will still be unfinished?
A. 3/10
B. 10/7
C. 7/3
D. 7/10
Answer: 7/10
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
R=1-\frac{3}{10}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
R=\frac{7}{10}=7/10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{10-3}{10}=7/10
\]
Answer: 7/10

Q487. A and B start a work together. A can complete it in 20 days and B in 26 days. After 5 days, A leaves. In how many days from the start will the work be completed?
A. 19 1/2 days
B. 22 1/2 days
C. 24 1/2 days
D. 23 2/5 days
Answer: 19 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(13+10)\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{145}{10}=19.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 19 1/2 days

Q488. Workers can complete a job in 8 days if there is no break. Work is paused for 3 days. What is the total elapsed time?

Family: tw_interrupted_work

\[

\]

\]

Q489. A repair team completes 18 metres per day and daily damage removes 3 metres. How long will 150 metres take?
A. 7 days
B. 12 days

D. 6 days
Answer: 10 days

Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:

\[

\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{150}{18-3}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 10 days

Q490. If 20 people can use a food stock for 36 days, how long will the stock last for 40 people?
A. 21 3/5 days
B. 23 days
C. 21 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 36=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 36}{40}
\]
Answer: 18 days

Q494. A job has 300 forms. 10 workers have finished 60 forms, and 12 days remain. How many more workers should be added?
A. 6 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 7 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{240}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q498. A work order has 120 forms. The first team completes 4 forms per day for 5 days, then the changed team completes 13 per day. What is the completion time?

B. 13.69 days
C. 16.69 days
D. 12.69 days
Answer: 12.69 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=4\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{100}{13}=12.69
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 12.69 days

Q499. A and B can separately complete a job in 18 days and 24 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 10.29 days
B. 18 days
C. 42 days
D. 21 days
Answer: 10.29 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(18,24)=72
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=72/18=4,\quad B=72/24=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{72}{4+3}=10.29
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{18\times 24}{18+24}=10.29
\]
Answer: 10.29 days

Q500. A and B together can complete a work in 8 days. A alone can complete it in 30 days. In how many days will B alone complete it?
A. 10.91 days
B. 38 days
C. 22 days
D. 19 days
Answer: 10.91 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{30}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
B=10.91
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{8\times 30}{30-8}=10.91
\]
Answer: 10.91 days
